---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

# Wildfire Bus Evacuation Planning

## Planning Goal

Create a fast-response evacuation policy for neighborhoods threatened by a
wildfire. The city has a fixed fleet of buses, several candidate staging depots,
candidate shelters, and evacuation corridors whose capacity and closure times
change across wildfire scenarios. Before the scenario is known, decide which
shelters to open, where to stage each bus, and which corridors receive
contraflow and traffic-control treatment. After a scenario is realized, route
each bus through a sequence of pickup-to-shelter trips and decide how many
people, wheelchair passengers, and pets remain unserved or shelter in place.

You are supporting an emergency operations center that must prepare a
bus-based evacuation plan for wildfire-threatened neighborhoods. Work inside
this task directory, read the full problem statement and every file under
`data/`, then create:

The goal is to minimize the risk-adjusted operating cost. Costs include shelter
opening, bus staging, corridor treatment, bus operating time, smoke exposure,
late pickups, unserved demand, and a tail-risk surcharge for expensive
scenarios.

## Operational Rules

Every submitted scenario plan must account for all listed evacuation demand at
each pickup site: people are either transported by bus, marked unserved, or
assigned to shelter in place. Wheelchair and pet quantities are subsets of the
people at the same pickup site and must also be accounted for. Unserved and
shelter-in-place quantities are allowed but expensive and cannot violate the
minimum service floors.

Each bus starts from its staged depot in every scenario. A bus trip carries
evacuees from one pickup site to one open shelter. Later trips by the same bus
start from the shelter reached by the previous trip. The submitted departure
time is the time when the bus leaves the pickup site after boarding. The bus
must have enough time to travel from its current location to the pickup site,
board passengers, travel to the shelter, unload passengers, and continue to the
next trip. Buses must respect passenger capacity, wheelchair-slot capacity,
their release time, and their shift limit.

Routes use the evacuation corridor assigned to the pickup site. Scenario rows
give each corridor's travel multiplier, trip capacity, smoke risk, and latest
safe pickup-departure time. Activating contraflow on a corridor increases its
trip capacity, delays closure, and reduces travel time, but uses limited
budget. A trip departing from a pickup after the corridor's adjusted safe time
is invalid. The number of trips using a corridor in a scenario cannot exceed
its adjusted capacity.

Open shelters must respect total evacuee capacity, wheelchair support
capacity, pet capacity, medical-bed capacity, backup-power requirements, and
smoke-safety requirements. High-risk medical pickups may only send evacuees to
open shelters with backup power. Shelters with lower smoke-safety ratings may
be unusable in severe smoke scenarios.

Each pickup site has a priority class and service floor. For every scenario,
the transported fraction plus accepted shelter-in-place fraction must meet the
site's floor. In addition, high-priority and medical pickup sites cannot have a
served fraction more than the configured fairness gap below any standard or
remote site in the same scenario.

This task is designed for a short solve budget. A full mixed-integer model can
be valuable for assignments, shelter choices, or repair steps, but a practical
submission may need scenario-wise construction, route balancing, local search,
and feasibility repair to produce a good policy quickly.

## Data Dictionary

`data/config.json`

- global planning budget, maximum open shelters, maximum activated corridors,
  loading/unloading times, risk weights, service-floor settings, and numerical
  tolerance.

`data/depots.csv`

- candidate staging depots, staging capacity, staging cost, release delay, and
  map coordinates.

`data/buses.csv`

- bus capacity, wheelchair slots, home depot, shift limit, operating cost, and
  special equipment flags.

`data/shelters.csv`

- shelter capacity, wheelchair support, pet capacity, medical beds, backup
  power, smoke-safety rating, opening cost, and map coordinates.

`data/pickup_sites.csv`

- pickup demand points, priority class, corridor, service floor, boarding bay
  limit, medical share, shelter-in-place limit, and map coordinates.

`data/corridors.csv`

- candidate evacuation corridors, treatment cost, travel-time reduction,
  closure extension, and capacity bonus if activated.

`data/scenarios.csv`

- wildfire scenario probability, smoke severity, global travel multiplier, and
  scenario-level warning time.

`data/scenario_pickup_demand.csv`

- scenario-specific evacuee, wheelchair, and pet demand at each pickup site.

`data/scenario_corridor_impacts.csv`

- scenario-corridor travel multiplier, trip capacity, smoke-risk multiplier,
  and latest safe pickup-departure time before contraflow adjustment.

## Submission Format

Create `submissions/solution.json` with this top-level structure:

```json
{
  "shelters": [{"shelter_id": "S1", "open": true}],
  "staging": [{"bus_id": "B01", "depot_id": "D1"}],
  "contraflow": [{"corridor_id": "C1", "active": true}],
  "scenario_plans": [
    {
      "scenario_id": "SLOW_WIND",
      "bus_trips": [
        {
          "bus_id": "B01",
          "sequence": 1,
          "pickup_id": "P01",
          "shelter_id": "S1",
          "depart_min": 55,
          "evacuees": 48,
          "wheelchair": 2,
          "pets": 4
        }
      ],
      "unserved": [
        {"pickup_id": "P01", "evacuees": 0, "wheelchair": 0, "pets": 0}
      ],
      "shelter_in_place": [
        {"pickup_id": "P01", "evacuees": 0, "wheelchair": 0, "pets": 0}
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

Include every bus in `staging`, every shelter in `shelters`, every corridor in
`contraflow`, and every scenario in `scenario_plans`. Each scenario must
include an `unserved` row and a `shelter_in_place` row for every pickup site,
even when the quantities are zero.

/bin/bash -lc "python - <<'PY'
import json, csv, math
from collections import defaultdict, Counter

# Load data
with open('/app/data/config.json') as f:
    config=json.load(f)

def load_csv(path, key=None):
    with open(path, newline='') as f:
        rows=list(csv.DictReader(f))
    if key:
        return {r[key]: r for r in rows}
    return rows

depots=load_csv('/app/data/depots.csv','depot_id')
buses=load_csv('/app/data/buses.csv','bus_id')
shelters=load_csv('/app/data/shelters.csv','shelter_id')
pickups=load_csv('/app/data/pickup_sites.csv','pickup_id')
corridors=load_csv('/app/data/corridors.csv','corridor_id')
scenarios=load_csv('/app/data/scenarios.csv','scenario_id')
spd=load_csv('/app/data/scenario_pickup_demand.csv')
imp=load_csv('/app/data/scenario_corridor_impacts.csv')

with open('/app/submissions/solution.json') as f:
    sol=json.load(f)

# Cast helpers
for d in [depots,buses,shelters,pickups,corridors,scenarios]:
    for k,row in d.items():
        for kk,v in list(row.items()):
            try:
                if v.strip()=='' :
                    continue
                if '.' in v or 'e' in v.lower():
                    row[kk]=float(v)
                else:
                    row[kk]=int(v)
            except Exception:
                pass

imp_map={(r['scenario_id'],r['corridor_id']): {k:(float(v) if k not in ('scenario_id','corridor_id') else v) for k,v in r.items()} for r in imp}
dem_map={(r['scenario_id'],r['pickup_id']): {k:(float(v) if k not in ('scenario_id','pickup_id') else v) for k,v in r.items()} for r in spd}

open_shelters={r['shelter_id']: r['open'] for r in sol['shelters']}
staging={r['bus_id']: r['depot_id'] for r in sol['staging']}
contraflow={r['corridor_id']: r['active'] for r in sol['contraflow']}

coords={}
for d in depots.values(): coords[d['depot_id']]=(d['x'],d['y'])
for d in shelters.values(): coords[d['shelter_id']]=(d['x'],d['y'])
for d in pickups.values(): coords[d['pickup_id']]=(d['x'],d['y'])

def dist(a,b):
    ax,ay=coords[a]; bx,by=coords[b]
    return math.hypot(ax-bx, ay-by)

def leg_time(src,dst,scen_id,pickup_id):
    # use current pickup's corridor characteristics for the leg associated with that pickup
    corridor_id=pickups[pickup_id]['corridor_id']
    m=scenarios[scen_id]['global_travel_multiplier'] * imp_map[(scen_id,corridor_id)]['travel_multiplier']
    if contraflow[corridor_id]:
        m *= corridors[corridor_id]['travel_time_factor_if_active']
    return (dist(src,dst)*config['minutes_per_distance_unit'] + config['base_intersection_delay_min']) * m

def board_time(ev,w):
    return ev*config['load_time_per_person_min'] + w*config['wheelchair_extra_board_min']

def unload_time(ev):
    return ev*config['unload_time_per_person_min']

viol=[]
# quick checks and timing slacks summary
for sp in sol['scenario_plans']:
    sid=sp['scenario_id']
    bus_trips=defaultdict(list)
    for t in sp['bus_trips']:
        bus_trips[t['bus_id']].append(t)
    for b,trips in bus_trips.items():
        trips=sorted(trips,key=lambda x:x['sequence'])
        loc=staging[b]
        t0=scenarios[sid]['warning_time_min'] + depots[staging[b]]['release_delay_min']
        for idx,tr in enumerate(trips):
            lb=t0 + leg_time(loc,tr['pickup_id'],sid,tr['pickup_id']) + board_time(tr['evacuees'], tr['wheelchair'])
            slack=tr['depart_min']-lb
            if slack < -1e-6:
                viol.append((sid,b,tr['sequence'],'depart_lb',tr['depart_min'],lb))
            t0=tr['depart_min'] + leg_time(tr['pickup_id'],tr['shelter_id'],sid,tr['pickup_id']) + unload_time(tr['evacuees'])
            loc=tr['shelter_id']
        if trips:
            finish=t0
            limit=scenarios[sid]['warning_time_min'] + depots[staging[b]]['release_delay_min'] + buses[b]['shift_limit_min']
            if finish-limit > 1e-6:
                viol.append((sid,b,'finish','shift',finish,limit))

print('timing viol count', len(viol))
for v in viol[:20]:
    print(v)

/bin/bash -lc "python - <<'PY'
import json, csv, math, statistics
from collections import defaultdict, Counter

TOL=1e-6

# Load public data
with open('/app/data/config.json') as f:
    config=json.load(f)


def load_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline='') as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))


def coerce_table(rows, id_cols=()):
    out=[]
    for r in rows:
        rr={}
        for k,v in r.items():
            if k in id_cols:
                rr[k]=v
                continue
            try:
                if v == '' or v is None:
                    rr[k]=v
                elif '.' in v or 'e' in v.lower():
                    rr[k]=float(v)
                else:
                    rr[k]=int(v)
            except Exception:
                rr[k]=v
        out.append(rr)
    return out

# tables
D={r['depot_id']:r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/depots.csv'),('depot_id','name'))}
B={r['bus_id']:r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/buses.csv'),('bus_id','home_depot'))}
S={r['shelter_id']:r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/shelters.csv'),('shelter_id','name'))}
P={r['pickup_id']:r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/pickup_sites.csv'),('pickup_id','name','priority_class','corridor_id'))}
C={r['corridor_id']:r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/corridors.csv'),('corridor_id','name'))}
Sc={r['scenario_id']:r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/scenarios.csv'),('scenario_id','description'))}
Dem={(r['scenario_id'],r['pickup_id']):r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/scenario_pickup_demand.csv'),('scenario_id','pickup_id'))}
Imp={(r['scenario_id'],r['corridor_id']):r for r in coerce_table(load_csv('/app/data/scenario_corridor_impacts.csv'),('scenario_id','corridor_id'))}

with open('/app/submissions/solution.json') as f:
    sol=json.load(f)

coords={**{k:(v['x'],v['y']) for k,v in D.items()}, **{k:(v['x'],v['y']) for k,v in S.items()}, **{k:(v['x'],v['y']) for k,v in P.items()}}

def euclid(a,b):
    ax,ay=coords[a]; bx,by=coords[b]
    return math.hypot(ax-bx, ay-by)

def corridor_mult(scenario_id,pickup_id,active_corridors):
    cid=P[pickup_id]['corridor_id']
    m=Sc[scenario_id]['global_travel_multiplier']*Imp[(scenario_id,cid)]['travel_multiplier']
    if active_corridors[cid]:
        m*=C[cid]['travel_time_factor_if_active']
    return m

def leg_time(src,dst,scenario_id,pickup_id,active_corridors):
    return (euclid(src,dst)*config['minutes_per_distance_unit']+config['base_intersection_delay_min'])*corridor_mult(scenario_id,pickup_id,active_corridors)

def board_time(ev,w):
    return ev*config['load_time_per_person_min'] + w*config['wheelchair_extra_board_min']

def unload_time(ev):
    return ev*config['unload_time_per_person_min']

def f(x):
    if isinstance(x,bool): return x
    return float(x)

viol=[]
covered=[]

# exact rows as exported
shelter_rows=sol.get('shelters',[])
staging_rows=sol.get('staging',[])
contra_rows=sol.get('contraflow',[])
scenario_rows=sol.get('scenario_plans',[])

# Schema and key coverage
covered.append(('schema/nullability and key coverage','top-level and required per-entity coverage'))
expected_shelters=set(S)
expected_buses=set(B)
expected_corridors=set(C)
expected_scenarios=set(Sc)
expected_pickups=set(P)

# top-level exact coverage
for name, rows, key, expected in [
    ('shelters', shelter_rows, 'shelter_id', expected_shelters),
    ('staging', staging_rows, 'bus_id', expected_buses),
    ('contraflow', contra_rows, 'corridor_id', expected_corridors),
    ('scenario_plans', scenario_rows, 'scenario_id', expected_scenarios),
]:
    seen=[r.get(key) for r in rows]
    dup=[k for k,v in Counter(seen).items() if v>1]
    miss=sorted(expected-set(seen))
    extra=sorted(set(seen)-expected)
    if dup:
        viol.append((name,'duplicate-key',dup,None,'unique required','remove duplicates'))
    if miss:
        viol.append((name,'missing-key',miss,None,'all public keys required','add missing rows'))
    if extra:
        viol.append((name,'unknown-key',extra,None,'identifier must exist in public data','remove or fix IDs'))

for sp in scenario_rows:
    sid=sp['scenario_id']
    for table in ('unserved','shelter_in_place'):
        rows=sp.get(table,[])
        seen=[r.get('pickup_id') for r in rows]
        dup=[k for k,v in Counter(seen).items() if v>1]
        miss=sorted(expected_pickups-set(seen))
        extra=sorted(set(seen)-expected_pickups)
        if dup:
            viol.append((f'{sid}.{table}','duplicate-pickup',dup,None,'unique pickup rows required','deduplicate rows'))
        if miss:
            viol.append((f'{sid}.{table}','missing-pickup',miss,None,'every pickup row required','add zero rows as needed'))
        if extra:
            viol.append((f'{sid}.{table}','unknown-pickup',extra,None,'pickup must exist in public data','remove or fix IDs'))

# nullability / domains
covered.append(('identifier domains','IDs, booleans, numeric signs, known domains'))
open_map={}
for r in shelter_rows:
    if r['shelter_id'] in S:
        open_map[r['shelter_id']]=r['open']
        if not isinstance(r['open'], bool):
            viol.append((('shelters',r['shelter_id']),'open-type',r['open'],None,'boolean','use true/false'))
for r in staging_rows:
    if r['bus_id'] in B and r['depot_id'] not in D:
        viol.append((('staging',r['bus_id']),'unknown-depot',r['depot_id'],None,'known depot_id','fix depot assignment'))
stage_map={r['bus_id']:r['depot_id'] for r in staging_rows if r.get('bus_id') in B and r.get('depot_id') in D}
active_map={}
for r in contra_rows:
    if r['corridor_id'] in C:
        active_map[r['corridor_id']]=r['active']
        if not isinstance(r['active'], bool):
            viol.append((('contraflow',r['corridor_id']),'active-type',r['active'],None,'boolean','use true/false'))

# first-stage budgets and limits
covered.append(('frozen/nonanticipative decisions','same first-stage policy across scenarios; budget/opening/staging feasibility'))
if sum(1 for v in open_map.values() if v) > config['max_open_shelters'] + TOL:
    viol.append((('shelters','open-count'),'max_open_shelters',sum(1 for v in open_map.values() if v),None,config['max_open_shelters'],'close shelters or open fewer'))
if sum(1 for v in active_map.values() if v) > config['max_active_corridors'] + TOL:
    viol.append((('contraflow','active-count'),'max_active_corridors',sum(1 for v in active_map.values() if v),None,config['max_active_corridors'],'deactivate corridors'))
backup_open=sum(1 for sid,v in open_map.items() if v and S[sid]['backup_power']==1)
if backup_open + TOL < config['min_backup_power_shelters']:
    viol.append((('shelters','backup-count'),'min_backup_power_shelters',backup_open,None,config['min_backup_power_shelters'],'open more backup-power shelters'))
for depot_id in D:
    count=sum(1 for bus_id,dd in stage_map.items() if dd==depot_id)
    if count > D[depot_id]['stage_capacity'] + TOL:
        viol.append((('staging',depot_id),'stage_capacity',count,None,D[depot_id]['stage_capacity'],'reassign buses to other depots'))
# planning budget
planning_cost=sum(S[sid]['open_cost'] for sid,v in open_map.items() if v) + sum(D[stage_map[b]]['stage_cost_per_bus'] for b in B if b in stage_map) + sum(C[cid]['activation_cost'] for cid,v in active_map.items() if v)
if planning_cost > config['planning_budget'] + TOL:
    viol.append((('first-stage','planning_budget'),'budget',planning_cost,None,config['planning_budget'],'reduce shelter openings, staging cost, or contraflow activations'))

# scenario checks
covered.extend([
    ('conservation and inventory','pickup demand balance for evacuees, wheelchair, pets; subset checks'),
    ('time/location/readiness/setup/non-overlap','bus sequencing, closure times, shift limits, boarding bay overlap'),
    ('capacities and scenario derates','bus, corridor, shelter, backup-power, smoke-rating, medical-bed limits'),
    ('reserves/ramps','service floors, shelter-in-place caps, fairness gap'),
    ('objective accounting','recomputed public cost components; no exported objective field to compare')
])

scenario_costs={}
for sp in scenario_rows:
    sid=sp['scenario_id']
    scen=Sc[sid]
    trips=sp['bus_trips']
    # domain and sequence checks
    per_bus=defaultdict(list)
    per_pickup_transport=defaultdict(lambda: {'evacuees':0.0,'wheelchair':0.0,'pets':0.0})
    per_shelter=defaultdict(lambda: {'evacuees':0.0,'wheelchair':0.0,'pets':0.0,'medical':0.0})
    corridor_trip_counts=Counter()
    bus_operating=0.0
    smoke_cost=0.0
    late_cost=0.0

    for tr in trips:
        key=(sid,tr['bus_id'],tr['sequence'])
        # id domains
        for field,dom in [('bus_id',B),('pickup_id',P),('shelter_id',S)]:
            if tr[field] not in dom:
                viol.append((key,f'unknown-{field}',tr[field],None,f'known {field}','fix identifier'))
        if tr['bus_id'] in B and tr['pickup_id'] in P and tr['shelter_id'] in S:
            per_bus[tr['bus_id']].append(tr)
            per_pickup_transport[tr['pickup_id']]['evacuees'] += tr['evacuees']
            per_pickup_transport[tr['pickup_id']]['wheelchair'] += tr['wheelchair']
            per_pickup_transport[tr['pickup_id']]['pets'] += tr['pets']
            per_shelter[tr['shelter_id']]['evacuees'] += tr['evacuees']
            per_shelter[tr['shelter_id']]['wheelchair'] += tr['wheelchair']
            per_shelter[tr['shelter_id']]['pets'] += tr['pets']
            per_shelter[tr['shelter_id']]['medical'] += tr['evacuees'] * P[tr['pickup_id']]['medical_share']
            cid=P[tr['pickup_id']]['corridor_id']
            corridor_trip_counts[cid]+=1
            # trip-level capacity/subset
            if tr['evacuees'] > B[tr['bus_id']]['capacity'] + TOL:
                viol.append((key,'bus_capacity',tr['evacuees'],None,B[tr['bus_id']]['capacity'],'reduce load or use larger bus'))
            if tr['wheelchair'] > B[tr['bus_id']]['wheelchair_slots'] + TOL:
                viol.append((key,'wheelchair_slots',tr['wheelchair'],None,B[tr['bus_id']]['wheelchair_slots'],'reduce wheelchair load or reassign bus'))
            if tr['wheelchair'] > tr['evacuees'] + TOL:
                viol.append((key,'wheelchair-subset',tr['wheelchair'],None,tr['evacuees'],'wheelchair passengers must be a subset of evacuees'))
            if tr['pets'] > tr['evacuees'] + TOL:
                viol.append((key,'pets-subset',tr['pets'],None,tr['evacuees'],'pets must be a subset of evacuees'))
            # late closure
            latest=Imp[(sid,cid)]['latest_safe_departure_min'] + (C[cid]['closure_extension_min'] if active_map[cid] else 0)
            if tr['depart_min'] > latest + TOL:
                viol.append((key,'latest_safe_departure',tr['depart_min'],None,latest,'move the trip earlier or activate/retain more closure extension'))
            # route costs
            smoke_mult=Imp[(sid,cid)]['smoke_risk_multiplier']
            smoke_cost += tr['evacuees'] * config['smoke_exposure_cost_per_person'] * smoke_mult
            late_cost += max(0.0, tr['depart_min'] - Imp[(sid,cid)]['latest_safe_departure_min']) * tr['evacuees'] * config['late_pickup_penalty_per_person_min']
        # numeric signs
        for field in ('sequence','depart_min','evacuees','wheelchair','pets'):
            if tr[field] is None:
                viol.append((key,f'null-{field}',None,None,'non-null','populate field'))
        if tr['sequence'] <= 0 or int(tr['sequence']) "'!= tr['"'sequence']:
            viol.append((key,'sequence-domain',tr['sequence'],None,'positive integer','renumber sequence'))
        for field in ('depart_min','evacuees','wheelchair','pets'):
            if tr[field] < -TOL:
                viol.append((key,f'negative-{field}',tr[field],None,0,'use nonnegative quantity'))

    # bus sequencing / exact arc simulation
    for bus_id,btrips in per_bus.items():
        ordered=sorted(btrips,key=lambda r:r['sequence'])
        seqs=[t['sequence'] for t in ordered]
        if seqs "'!= list(range(1,len(ordered)+1)):

        current_loc=stage_map[bus_id]
        earliest=scen['warning_time_min'] + D[stage_map[bus_id]]['release_delay_min']
        for i,tr in enumerate(ordered):
            required_depart = earliest + leg_time(current_loc,tr['pickup_id'],sid,tr['pickup_id'],active_map) + board_time(tr['evacuees'], tr['wheelchair'])
            if tr['depart_min'] + TOL < required_depart:
                viol.append(((sid,bus_id,tr['sequence']),'route-timing',tr['depart_min'],None,required_depart,'delay this departure or alter prior arcs/load'))
            # operating time counts both legs + service
            bus_operating += leg_time(current_loc,tr['pickup_id'],sid,tr['pickup_id'],active_map) + board_time(tr['evacuees'], tr['wheelchair'])
            bus_operating += leg_time(tr['pickup_id'],tr['shelter_id'],sid,tr['pickup_id'],active_map) + unload_time(tr['evacuees'])
            earliest = tr['depart_min'] + leg_time(tr['pickup_id'],tr['shelter_id'],sid,tr['pickup_id'],active_map) + unload_time(tr['evacuees'])
            current_loc = tr['shelter_id']
        finish=earliest
        shift_end=scen['warning_time_min'] + D[stage_map[bus_id]]['release_delay_min'] + B[bus_id]['shift_limit_min']
        if finish > shift_end + TOL:
            viol.append(((sid,bus_id),'shift_limit',finish,None,shift_end,'shorten route or reduce waiting/trips'))

    # pickup balances and limits
    unserved={r['pickup_id']:r for r in sp['unserved']}

        dem=Dem[(sid,pid)]

                viol.append(((sid,rowname,pid),'pets-subset',row['pets'],None,row['evacuees'],'pet count must be a subset of evacuees'))

exp=sum(probs[sid]*scenario_costs[sid]['scenario_total'] for sid in Sc)
alpha=config['tail_risk_alpha']
# CVaR-style surcharge based on weighted scenarios
ordered=sorted(((scenario_costs[sid]['scenario_total'], probs[sid], sid) for sid in Sc), reverse=True)
remaining=1-alpha
cvar_num=0.0
for cost,p,sid in ordered:
    take=min(remaining,p)
    if take>0:
        cvar_num += take*cost
        remaining -= take
if 1-alpha>0:
    cvar=cvar_num/(1-alpha)
else:
    cvar=max(c['scenario_total'] for c in scenario_costs.values())
print('EXPECTED_SCENARIO_COST', exp)
print('TAIL_CVAR', cvar)
print('RISK_ADJUSTED_TOTAL', first_stage + exp + config['tail_risk_weight']*cvar)

---
name: optimization-modeling-core
description: Use for natural-language-to-optimization tasks that require translating a business problem into a complete mathematical model before writing solver code.
metadata:
  short-description: Turn business text into a full optimization model
---

# Optimization Modeling Core

## Use when

- The user gives a word problem, business process, paper scenario, or benchmark prompt and expects a formal optimization model.
- Solver code exists but the issue appears to be a missing or incorrect formulation.
- The task asks for a complete model, not only a heuristic, simulation, or data summary.

## Do not use when

- The task is purely about software plumbing, visualization, data cleaning, or report writing.
- The problem is already fully formulated and the user only asks for code translation into a specific solver; use the solver-specific skill as the primary guide.
- The request is exploratory and the user explicitly wants brainstorming without implementation.

## Model-first workflow

1. Restate the business decision in plain language.
2. Identify sets, indices, units, and time granularity.
3. Extract parameters from data files and confirm units.
4. Define decision variables with domains and business meaning.
5. Write the objective with every cost, reward, penalty, and service term.
6. Write constraints grouped by operational rule.
7. Check edge cases: missing eligibility, impossible demand, fixed commitments, and terminal-state requirements.
8. Only then implement code.

## Ambiguity handling

- If the brief is ambiguous but data and examples imply a convention, state the assumption and continue.
- If ambiguity changes feasibility, objective direction, or deliverable schema, ask before coding or implement the least risky interpretation with a clear note.
- If units conflict across files, normalize units explicitly before modeling.
- If a hard requirement conflicts with data, diagnose the conflict before adding slack variables.

## Formulation checklist

- Every row in an input table should either become a parameter, an eligibility rule, or be explicitly irrelevant.
- Every decision variable should appear in at least one binding constraint and usually in the objective.
- Demand balance should use the correct equality or inequality direction; do not weaken equality to `>=` unless surplus is genuinely allowed.
- Capacity constraints should include activation/open/setup binaries when the business says inactive resources cannot produce, ship, or serve.
- Shortage, backlog, overtime, outsourcing, and postponement variables need explicit cost and bounds.
- Multi-period models need state transition equations and initial/terminal states.
- If the user asks for explanation, describe the modeling logic in business terms after solving.

## Constraint boundary rules

- Hard constraints represent physical impossibility, policy mandates, legal requirements, or explicit benchmark feasibility rules.
- Soft constraints need penalty variables or objective terms; do not silently turn hard constraints into penalties.
- Service levels should specify numerator, denominator, eligible population, and time window.
- Fairness constraints should define the comparison group and whether unavailable resources count.
- Logical implications need both directions only when the business rule says equivalence, not merely implication.

## Objective boundary rules

- Confirm whether the problem minimizes cost, maximizes profit, maximizes service, or uses lexicographic priorities.
- Do not mix penalties with primary economics unless their scale is intentional.
- Include fixed costs only once per activated entity.
- Include revenue only for fulfilled demand or accepted jobs, not for unmet or canceled work.
- Keep reporting metrics separate from optimized objective terms unless the prompt explicitly optimizes them.

## Implementation expectations

- Use structured parsing for CSV/JSON instead of manual string manipulation.
- Keep solver code, solution export, and validation code clearly separated.
- For benchmark or task environments, read the provided brief before coding and follow the expected output schema exactly.
- When comparing two answers, diagnose concrete formulation differences and objective consequences, not only feasibility.
- Write exported solutions at the precision expected by the checker; avoid unnecessary rounding.
- Preserve the input schema and output schema unless the user asks to redesign the benchmark.

## Verification boundary

- Verify feasibility independently from solver status when an evaluator or checker exists.
- Recompute objective from exported artifacts, not only from in-memory solver expressions.
- For benchmark tasks, create at least one negative check for each newly added rule when practical.
- For paper or real-world tasks, separate what is directly supported by the source from synthetic or assumed data.

## Red flags

- Objective terms that are counted twice or charged at the wrong marginal rate.
- Binary variables not linked tightly enough to continuous activity.
- Time periods shifted by one index.
- Average or fairness constraints that ignore the denominator or eligibility set.
- Public prompt files that reveal solver-ready variables or formulas when the benchmark is meant to test modeling translation.
- A feasible solution that exploits missing eligibility, free disposal, negative cycles, or unbounded production.
- A model that reports optimality after a time limit, infeasible status, or missing incumbent.

# OR PySCIPOpt

## Use when

- The task asks for a Python optimization model using SCIP or `pyscipopt`.
- Existing solver code imports `pyscipopt`, sets SCIP parameters, writes `.lp`/`.mps`, or calls a SCIP-based checker.
- The user asks to run, debug, speed up, validate, or explain a SCIP-based solve.

## Do not use when

- The task only needs a mathematical formulation and no solver-specific code.
- The project clearly uses a different solver stack, such as Pyomo with CBC/HiGHS, OR-Tools CP-SAT, PuLP, CPLEX, or cvxpy, unless the user asks to port it to PySCIPOpt.
- The model is nonlinear or stochastic in a way that SCIP cannot handle directly; first decide whether it can be linearized or approximated.

## Environment rule

Use the Python environment specified by the user or the project. In this ORClaw workspace, the default solver environment is the `or` conda environment with PySCIPOpt available:

```bash
conda run -n or python <script.py>
```

If `pyscipopt` is missing, report the missing dependency, identify the command that failed, and try the project-specified environment before changing solver stacks.

## Workflow

1. Read the task brief and input files before coding.
2. Write the full mathematical model first: sets, parameters, decision variables, objective, and constraints.
3. Implement the model with explicit tuple keys and named constraints.
4. Solve with the selected Python command.
5. Export the solution using the exact schema required by the task.
6. Validate with the task checker or an independent recomputation of feasibility and objective.
7. If the solution is not accepted, diagnose whether the issue is formulation, data parsing, export formatting, tolerance, or evaluator mismatch before changing the objective or constraints.

## PySCIPOpt patterns

- Use `from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum`.
- Create sparse variable dictionaries explicitly, for example:

```python
x = {(i, j, t): model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"x[{i},{j},{t}]") for i, j, t in keys}
```

- Use `quicksum(...)` for large linear expressions.
- Use `model.addCons(expr <= rhs, name="capacity[k,t]")` for constraints.
- Use `model.setObjective(expr, "minimize")` or `model.setObjective(expr, "maximize")`.
- Set reproducibility and limits with SCIP parameters:

```python
model.setParam("limits/gap", 0.0005)
model.setParam("limits/time", time_limit)
model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
```

- After `model.optimize()`, inspect `model.getStatus()` and only read values if a solution exists.
- Read variable values with `model.getVal(x[key])`, not `x[key].X`.
- Read objective and bound with `model.getObjVal()` and `model.getDualbound()`.
- Read relative gap with `model.getGap()` when available.

## Modeling hygiene

- Calibrate every big-M from data. If a tight M is not obvious, derive a bound and document it in a short comment.
- Keep feasibility checks, objective recomputation, and reference-optimality comparison separate.
- Do not round exported decision values unless the schema explicitly requires it.
- For benchmark work, treat the evaluator or checker as the scoring authority and the reference solver as a validation baseline.
- Keep solver logs or a concise solve summary when the user needs auditability.
- Avoid changing data to make a model feasible unless the task is explicitly to design or repair an instance.

## Debugging boundaries

- If a model is infeasible, inspect hard lower bounds, conservation equations, and mutually exclusive binary logic before relaxing constraints.
- If objective values differ, recompute each objective component from exported decisions.
- If runtime is high, first remove dominated variables, tighten big-M values, add eligibility filters, and check whether binaries are really necessary.
- If SCIP returns an incumbent with a gap, say so clearly; do not call it exact optimality unless the status and gap support that claim.
- If an evaluator rejects a solution that the model considers feasible, replay the rejected rule directly from the exported file before changing solver code.

## Useful commands

```bash
python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
conda run -n or python -c "import pyscipopt; print(pyscipopt.__version__)"
<python-command> <solver-script.py>
<python-command> <checker-script.py> --solution <solution-file> --env-dir <task-dir>
```

# Wildfire Bus Evacuation Planning

## Planning Goal

Create a fast-response evacuation policy for neighborhoods threatened by a
wildfire. The city has a fixed fleet of buses, several candidate staging depots,
candidate shelters, and evacuation corridors whose capacity and closure times
change across wildfire scenarios. Before the scenario is known, decide which
shelters to open, where to stage each bus, and which corridors receive
contraflow and traffic-control treatment. After a scenario is realized, route
each bus through a sequence of pickup-to-shelter trips and decide how many
people, wheelchair passengers, and pets remain unserved or shelter in place.

You are supporting an emergency operations center that must prepare a
bus-based evacuation plan for wildfire-threatened neighborhoods. Work inside
this task directory, read the full problem statement and every file under
`data/`, then create:

The goal is to minimize the risk-adjusted operating cost. Costs include shelter
opening, bus staging, corridor treatment, bus operating time, smoke exposure,
late pickups, unserved demand, and a tail-risk surcharge for expensive
scenarios.

## Operational Rules

Every submitted scenario plan must account for all listed evacuation demand at
each pickup site: people are either transported by bus, marked unserved, or
assigned to shelter in place. Wheelchair and pet quantities are subsets of the
people at the same pickup site and must also be accounted for. Unserved and
shelter-in-place quantities are allowed but expensive and cannot violate the
minimum service floors.

Each bus starts from its staged depot in every scenario. A bus trip carries
evacuees from one pickup site to one open shelter. Later trips by the same bus
start from the shelter reached by the previous trip. The submitted departure
time is the time when the bus leaves the pickup site after boarding. The bus
must have enough time to travel from its current location to the pickup site,
board passengers, travel to the shelter, unload passengers, and continue to the
next trip. Buses must respect passenger capacity, wheelchair-slot capacity,
their release time, and their shift limit.

Routes use the evacuation corridor assigned to the pickup site. Scenario rows
give each corridor's travel multiplier, trip capacity, smoke risk, and latest
safe pickup-departure time. Activating contraflow on a corridor increases its
trip capacity, delays closure, and reduces travel time, but uses limited
budget. A trip departing from a pickup after the corridor's adjusted safe time
is invalid. The number of trips using a corridor in a scenario cannot exceed
its adjusted capacity.

Open shelters must respect total evacuee capacity, wheelchair support
capacity, pet capacity, medical-bed capacity, backup-power requirements, and
smoke-safety requirements. High-risk medical pickups may only send evacuees to
open shelters with backup power. Shelters with lower smoke-safety ratings may
be unusable in severe smoke scenarios.

Each pickup site has a priority class and service floor. For every scenario,
the transported fraction plus accepted shelter-in-place fraction must meet the
site's floor. In addition, high-priority and medical pickup sites cannot have a
served fraction more than the configured fairness gap below any standard or
remote site in the same scenario.

This task is designed for a short solve budget. A full mixed-integer model can
be valuable for assignments, shelter choices, or repair steps, but a practical
submission may need scenario-wise construction, route balancing, local search,
and feasibility repair to produce a good policy quickly.

## Data Dictionary

`data/config.json`

- global planning budget, maximum open shelters, maximum activated corridors,
  loading/unloading times, risk weights, service-floor settings, and numerical
  tolerance.

`data/depots.csv`

- candidate staging depots, staging capacity, staging cost, release delay, and
  map coordinates.

`data/buses.csv`

- bus capacity, wheelchair slots, home depot, shift limit, operating cost, and
  special equipment flags.

`data/shelters.csv`

- shelter capacity, wheelchair support, pet capacity, medical beds, backup
  power, smoke-safety rating, opening cost, and map coordinates.

`data/pickup_sites.csv`

- pickup demand points, priority class, corridor, service floor, boarding bay
  limit, medical share, shelter-in-place limit, and map coordinates.

`data/corridors.csv`

- candidate evacuation corridors, treatment cost, travel-time reduction,
  closure extension, and capacity bonus if activated.

`data/scenarios.csv`

- wildfire scenario probability, smoke severity, global travel multiplier, and
  scenario-level warning time.

`data/scenario_pickup_demand.csv`

- scenario-specific evacuee, wheelchair, and pet demand at each pickup site.

`data/scenario_corridor_impacts.csv`

- scenario-corridor travel multiplier, trip capacity, smoke-risk multiplier,
  and latest safe pickup-departure time before contraflow adjustment.

## Submission Format

Create `submissions/solution.json` with this top-level structure:

```json
{
  "shelters": [{"shelter_id": "S1", "open": true}],
  "staging": [{"bus_id": "B01", "depot_id": "D1"}],
  "contraflow": [{"corridor_id": "C1", "active": true}],
  "scenario_plans": [
    {
      "scenario_id": "SLOW_WIND",
      "bus_trips": [
        {
          "bus_id": "B01",
          "sequence": 1,
          "pickup_id": "P01",
          "shelter_id": "S1",
          "depart_min": 55,
          "evacuees": 48,
          "wheelchair": 2,
          "pets": 4
        }
      ],
      "unserved": [
        {"pickup_id": "P01", "evacuees": 0, "wheelchair": 0, "pets": 0}
      ],
      "shelter_in_place": [
        {"pickup_id": "P01", "evacuees": 0, "wheelchair": 0, "pets": 0}
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

Include every bus in `staging`, every shelter in `shelters`, every corridor in
`contraflow`, and every scenario in `scenario_plans`. Each scenario must
include an `unserved` row and a `shelter_in_place` row for every pickup site,
even when the quantities are zero.

---
name: advanced-mip-patterns
description: Use when a task involves fixed charges, logical implications, min/max choices, sequencing, no-overlap, piecewise costs, routing-state logic, or other advanced MIP modeling patterns.
metadata:
  short-description: Advanced MIP formulation patterns
---

# Advanced MIP Patterns

## Use when

- The formulation needs logical implications, fixed charges, activation decisions, ordering, no-overlap, disjunctions, min/max, absolute values, tiers, route state, or resource calendars.
- A simple LP/MIP is producing unrealistic behavior because decisions are not linked tightly enough.
- The model is slow or infeasible and the likely cause is weak big-M logic, excessive pairwise variables, or missing state transitions.

## Do not use when

- A continuous LP is sufficient and adding binaries would not represent a real decision.
- The task is better handled by a specialized CP-SAT, constraint programming, nonlinear, or simulation method and the user did not require MIP.
- The user only needs a high-level explanation; do not introduce advanced constructs unless they clarify the actual model.

## General boundary rules

- Add binaries only for real yes/no choices, disjunctions, or nonconvex structure.
- Prefer the tightest formulation that matches the business rule; avoid decorative binaries.
- Use solver-native constructs when they improve clarity and are supported by the target environment.
- If a construct makes the model much slower, compare it with a simpler valid alternative before keeping it.

## Fixed charge and activation

- Use a binary `y` for whether an activity/resource is active.
- Link activity with a tight bound: `x <= U * y`.
- Add lower activation only when the business requires minimum use: `x >= L * y`.
- Charge fixed cost once through `fixed_cost * y`, not per unit.
- Do not force `y = 1` whenever `x = 0` unless the activity is mandatory.
- If there are startup/shutdown costs over time, separate active state from transition state.

## Logical rules

- Prefer solver-native indicator constraints for simple if-then rules when they keep the model clearer.
- If using big-M, derive M from data bounds and keep one M per rule family when possible.
- For either-or choices, use binaries whose sum is exactly one or at most one, matching the business rule.
- Distinguish implication (`A -> B`) from equivalence (`A <-> B`); the reverse direction often needs a separate constraint.
- For conditional capacity, link both activity and resource use to the condition.

## Min, max, absolute value

- For `z = max(a, b)`, add `z >= a`, `z >= b`, and objective pressure if minimizing z. Use binaries only when equality is needed without objective pressure.
- For absolute deviation `d = |x - target|`, add `d >= x - target` and `d >= target - x`.
- For service-level penalties, separate hard feasibility from soft penalty variables.
- For `min`, either transform with signs or add binaries when equality is not enforced by the objective.
- For ratio constraints, cross-multiply only when denominators are constant or safely bounded away from zero.

## Sequencing and no-overlap

- For jobs sharing a resource, use order binary `before[i,j]`.
- Typical pattern:
  - `start[j] >= end[i] - M * (1 - before[i,j])`
  - `start[i] >= end[j] - M * before[i,j]`
- Compute M from the scheduling horizon, not from an arbitrary large number.
- Include setup, cleanup, travel, rest, or turnover time in the correct side of the ordering constraint.
- Create pairwise order variables only for pairs that can share a resource or conflict.
- If time is discrete and the horizon is small, a time-indexed model may be cleaner than pairwise disjunctions.

## Piecewise and tiers

- Use solver-native piecewise-linear features for convex piecewise costs when suitable.
- For nonconvex tiers, use explicit segment variables and segment activation binaries.
- Ensure tier quantities sum to the original activity and each tier has its own upper bound.
- Distinguish incremental tiers from all-units tiers; their formulations and costs differ.
- Check whether discount tiers create nonconvex incentives that require binary selection.

## State and inventory logic

- State variables need initial conditions, transition equations, and terminal requirements if the business specifies them.
- Route or schedule state should be advanced in event order, not checked only row by row.
- Recovery, restock, cleaning, cooldown, maintenance, and rest rules often require cumulative state or last-event logic.
- If state can reset, model the reset trigger and its resource/time cost.

## Debugging advanced models

- First solve a relaxed or smaller instance and inspect whether the intended binaries activate.
- Add named constraints so IIS output points to business rules.
- If the reference solution is infeasible, replay the relevant state transitions manually before changing the evaluator.
- If the LP relaxation is very weak, inspect big-M values, missing lower/upper links, and symmetry.
- If the model is unexpectedly infeasible, test each advanced rule family on a tiny instance with a known feasible solution.

import csv, json, math
from pathlib import Path
root=Path('/app/data')
with open(root/'config.json') as f: cfg=json.load(f)
for name in ['depots','shelters','pickup_sites']:
    with open(root/f'{name}.csv') as f:
        rows=list(csv.DictReader(f))
    print(name, len(rows))
    globals()[name]=rows

def dist(a,b):
    return math.hypot(float(a['x'])-float(b['x']), float(a['y'])-float(b['y']))
mpd=cfg['minutes_per_distance_unit']; base=cfg['base_intersection_delay_min']
# shortest depot->pickup and pickup->shelter base travel
mins=[]
for p in pickup_sites:
    md=min(base+mpd*dist(d,p) for d in depots)
    ms=min(base+mpd*dist(p,s) for s in shelters)
    mins.append((p['pickup_id'], md, ms, md+ms))
print('pickup min depot/pickup->shelter totals:')
for row in mins: print(row)
print('shortest total', min(t for _,_,_,t in mins), 'largest shortest total', max(t for _,_,_,t in mins))
PY"

import csv
import json
import math
import os
import sys
import time
from collections import defaultdict
from pathlib import Path

from pyscipopt import Model, quicksum, __version__ as PYSCIPOPT_VERSION


ROOT = Path("/app")
DATA_DIR = ROOT / "data"
SUBMISSIONS_DIR = ROOT / "submissions"
SOLUTION_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solution.json"
LOG_PATH = SUBMISSIONS_DIR / "solve_log.md"

KMAX = 4
MIP_GAP = 0.0005
TIME_BUFFER_SECONDS = 15.0


def read_csv(path):
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        return list(csv.DictReader(f))


def as_int(value):
    return int(float(value))


def as_float(value):
    return float(value)


def q8(value):
    rounded = round(float(value) + 0.0, 8)
    if abs(rounded) < 1e-9:
        rounded = 0.0
    return rounded


def load_data():
    with open(DATA_DIR / "config.json", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        config = json.load(f)

    depots = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "depots.csv"):
        depots[row["depot_id"]] = {
            "depot_id": row["depot_id"],
            "name": row["name"],
            "stage_capacity": as_int(row["stage_capacity"]),
            "stage_cost_per_bus": as_float(row["stage_cost_per_bus"]),
            "release_delay_min": as_float(row["release_delay_min"]),
            "x": as_float(row["x"]),
            "y": as_float(row["y"]),
        }

    buses = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "buses.csv"):
        buses[row["bus_id"]] = {
            "bus_id": row["bus_id"],
            "home_depot": row["home_depot"],
            "capacity": as_int(row["capacity"]),
            "wheelchair_slots": as_int(row["wheelchair_slots"]),
            "shift_limit_min": as_float(row["shift_limit_min"]),
            "operating_cost_per_min": as_float(row["operating_cost_per_min"]),
            "high_clearance": as_int(row["high_clearance"]),
            "medical_attendant": as_int(row["medical_attendant"]),
        }

    shelters = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "shelters.csv"):
        shelters[row["shelter_id"]] = {
            "shelter_id": row["shelter_id"],
            "name": row["name"],
            "capacity": as_int(row["capacity"]),
            "wheelchair_capacity": as_int(row["wheelchair_capacity"]),
            "pet_capacity": as_int(row["pet_capacity"]),
            "medical_beds": as_float(row["medical_beds"]),
            "backup_power": as_int(row["backup_power"]),
            "smoke_safe_rating": as_int(row["smoke_safe_rating"]),
            "open_cost": as_float(row["open_cost"]),
            "x": as_float(row["x"]),
            "y": as_float(row["y"]),
        }

    pickups = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "pickup_sites.csv"):
        pickups[row["pickup_id"]] = {
            "pickup_id": row["pickup_id"],
            "name": row["name"],
            "priority_class": row["priority_class"],
            "corridor_id": row["corridor_id"],
            "service_floor": as_float(row["service_floor"]),
            "boarding_bays": as_int(row["boarding_bays"]),
            "medical_share": as_float(row["medical_share"]),
            "shelter_in_place_limit_fraction": as_float(row["shelter_in_place_limit_fraction"]),
            "x": as_float(row["x"]),
            "y": as_float(row["y"]),
        }

    corridors = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "corridors.csv"):
        corridors[row["corridor_id"]] = {
            "corridor_id": row["corridor_id"],
            "name": row["name"],
            "activation_cost": as_float(row["activation_cost"]),
            "travel_time_factor_if_active": as_float(row["travel_time_factor_if_active"]),
            "closure_extension_min": as_float(row["closure_extension_min"]),
            "capacity_bonus_trips": as_int(row["capacity_bonus_trips"]),
        }

    scenarios = {}
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenarios.csv"):
        scenarios[row["scenario_id"]] = {
            "scenario_id": row["scenario_id"],
            "probability": as_float(row["probability"]),
            "description": row["description"],
            "smoke_severity": as_float(row["smoke_severity"]),
            "global_travel_multiplier": as_float(row["global_travel_multiplier"]),
            "warning_time_min": as_float(row["warning_time_min"]),
            "min_shelter_smoke_rating": as_int(row["min_shelter_smoke_rating"]),
        }

    demand = defaultdict(dict)
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenario_pickup_demand.csv"):
        demand[row["scenario_id"]][row["pickup_id"]] = {
            "evacuees": as_int(row["evacuees"]),
            "wheelchair": as_int(row["wheelchair"]),
            "pets": as_int(row["pets"]),
        }

    corridor_impacts = defaultdict(dict)
    for row in read_csv(DATA_DIR / "scenario_corridor_impacts.csv"):
        corridor_impacts[row["scenario_id"]][row["corridor_id"]] = {
            "travel_multiplier": as_float(row["travel_multiplier"]),
            "capacity_trips": as_int(row["capacity_trips"]),
            "smoke_risk_multiplier": as_float(row["smoke_risk_multiplier"]),
            "latest_safe_departure_min": as_float(row["latest_safe_departure_min"]),
        }

    return {
        "config": config,
        "depots": depots,
        "buses": buses,
        "shelters": shelters,
        "pickups": pickups,
        "corridors": corridors,
        "scenarios": scenarios,
        "demand": demand,
        "corridor_impacts": corridor_impacts,
    }


def dist(a, b):
    return math.hypot(a["x"] - b["x"], a["y"] - b["y"])


def choose_first_stage(data):
    config = data["config"]
    shelters = data["shelters"]
    corridors = data["corridors"]
    depots = data["depots"]
    buses = data["buses"]
    pickups = data["pickups"]
    scenarios = data["scenarios"]
    demand = data["demand"]
    impacts = data["corridor_impacts"]

    open_shelters = sorted(
        shelter_id
        for shelter_id, shelter in shelters.items()
        if shelter["smoke_safe_rating"] >= 4
    )
    if len(open_shelters) != 4:
        raise RuntimeError("Expected exactly four smoke-safe shelters with rating >= 4.")

    active_corridors = sorted(corridors)
    if len(active_corridors) > config["max_active_corridors"]:
        raise RuntimeError("Instance-specific dominant contraflow choice exceeds active-corridor limit.")

    fixed_open_cost = sum(shelters[s]["open_cost"] for s in open_shelters)
    fixed_corridor_cost = sum(corridors[c]["activation_cost"] for c in active_corridors)

    model = Model("staging")
    model.setParam("display/verblevel", 0)
    model.setParam("limits/gap", MIP_GAP)

    z = {}
    for b in buses:
        for d in depots:
            z[b, d] = model.addVar(vtype="B", name=f"stage[{b},{d}]")

    proxy = {}
    for b, bus in buses.items():
        for d, depot in depots.items():
            weighted = 0.0
            for w, scenario in scenarios.items():
                total_dem = sum(demand[w][p]["evacuees"] for p in pickups)
                for p, pickup in pickups.items():
                    weight = scenario["probability"] * demand[w][p]["evacuees"] / total_dem
                    c = pickup["corridor_id"]
                    factor = (
                        scenario["global_travel_multiplier"]
                        * impacts[w][c]["travel_multiplier"]
                        * corridors[c]["travel_time_factor_if_active"]
                    )
                    base = (
                        config["base_intersection_delay_min"]
                        + config["minutes_per_distance_unit"] * dist(depot, pickup)
                    )
                    weighted += weight * factor * base
            proxy[b, d] = depots[d]["stage_cost_per_bus"] + bus["operating_cost_per_min"] * weighted

    for b in buses:
        model.addCons(quicksum(z[b, d] for d in depots) == 1, name=f"assign[{b}]")

    for d, depot in depots.items():
        model.addCons(
            quicksum(z[b, d] for b in buses) <= depot["stage_capacity"],
            name=f"depot_cap[{d}]",
        )

    model.addCons(
        quicksum(depots[d]["stage_cost_per_bus"] * z[b, d] for b in buses for d in depots)
        + fixed_open_cost
        + fixed_corridor_cost
        <= config["planning_budget"],
        name="planning_budget",
    )

    model.setObjective(quicksum(proxy[b, d] * z[b, d] for b in buses for d in depots), "minimize")
    model.optimize()

    if model.getNSols() == 0:
        raise RuntimeError("No feasible staging assignment found.")

    sol = model.getBestSol()
    staging = {}
    for b in buses:
        chosen = None
        for d in depots:
            if model.getSolVal(sol, z[b, d]) > 0.5:
                chosen = d
                break
        if chosen is None:
            raise RuntimeError(f"Staging missing for bus {b}.")
        staging[b] = chosen

    return {
        "open_shelters": open_shelters,
        "active_corridors": active_corridors,
        "staging": staging,
        "fixed_open_cost": fixed_open_cost,
        "fixed_corridor_cost": fixed_corridor_cost,
    }
tool55. Command (exit 0)
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '260,620p' /app/submissions/solve.py"
